Evidence type: Exact quotation
Exact quotation:
15. Right of appeal
15.1 A Student may appeal a decision of the Authorised Officer or Student Conduct Panel that Student
Misconduct has occurred and/or any Penalty imposed:
(a) within 7 days of a decision made or Penalty imposed under the Pathway 1 Process; or
(b) within 14 days of a decision made or Penalty imposed under the Pathway 2 Process.
15.2 In exceptional circumstances, the Academic Registrar may give a Student more time to appeal.

to the Head of School (or authorised officer) will do so within 10 working days of the date that
the mark for the Assessment Task was released to the student or publication date of the
official unit results, whichever is the earlier.

Document title: Student Misconduct Rules 2026
Printed bundle page: 200
Line numbers: L010–L015
Evidence type: Exact quotation
Exact quotation:
Academic Misconduct means conduct by a Student, other than conduct constituting Academic
Record Fraud or General Misconduct, that is dishonest or unfair in connection with any academic
work, such as:
(a) during any exam, test or other supervised assessment activity;
(b) in relation to the preparation or presentation of any assessed item of work; or
(c) in relation to the conduct of research or any other similar academic activity.

Principle 3: Fairness
Develop, deploy and use AI systems so there is no unfair
discrimination against individuals, communities or groups. Train and
test models used by AI Systems on relevant, accurate and
appropriate datasets. Train users to deploy and use AI systems
responsibly to manage and mitigate bias. Enable access, inclusion
and equity in AI systems throughout their lifecycle.

Record Fraud or General Misconduct, that is dishonest or unfair in connection with any academic
work, such as:
(a) during any exam, test or other supervised assessment activity;
(b) in relation to the preparation or presentation of any assessed item of work; or
(c) in relation to the conduct of research or any other similar academic activity.

Before any personal information is disclosed, staff receiving any request for the release of

seek information, unless otherwise authorised pursuant to these procedures.

2.2 Verification of identity and authority
Before any personal information is disclosed, staff receiving any request for the release of
personal information will:
(a) undertake a verification process with the requestor and assure themselves of the
requestor’s identity. This will be done at the discretion of the staff member to a
reasonable level and may be completed by sighting and confirming identifying
information; and
(b) where the request is made by a third party (that is, by someone who is not the person
who owns the personal information), obtain evidence of the requestor’s authority to
seek information, unless otherwise authorised pursuant to these procedures.

2.2 Verification of identity and authority
Before any personal information is disclosed, staff receiving any request for the release of
personal information will:
(a) undertake a verification process with the requestor and assure themselves of the
requestor’s identity. This will be done at the discretion of the staff member to a
reasonable level and may be completed by sighting and confirming identifying
information; and
(b) where the request is made by a third party (that is, by someone who is not the person
who owns the personal information), obtain evidence of the requestor’s authority to
seek information, unless otherwise authorised pursuant to these procedures.

recordkeeping obligations.

Document title: Artificial Intelligence (AI) Policy
Printed bundle page: 6
Line numbers: L020–L028
Evidence type: Exact quotation
Exact quotation:
Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has
been used and contest decisions made using AI.
Principle 7: Accountability
Implement human oversight, review and the ability to intervene for all AI-
based functions and decisions. Identify the individual accountable for any
decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.

Case note:

Source ID: A008
Document title: Curtin University AI Use in Research
Printed bundle page: 286
Line numbers: L008–L014
Evidence type: Exact quotation
Exact quotation:
AI tools can enhance research by quickly summarising or refining text, analysing large data sets, crafting and debugging code, communicating research
results and streamlining repetitive tasks. To use them well, researchers need to protect intellectual integrity and authorship, safeguard intellectual
property (including Indigenous Cultural and Intellectual Property), use data in culturally appropriate ways, and keep human oversight and expert
judgement at the centre of their work. AI tools must never substitute for human critical thinking, expertise and evaluation.
The guidelines apply to all Curtin researchers, including research students in master and doctoral programs. They cover generative AI tools for text,
images, audio, code and data, as well as generative AI-driven support platforms. They should be read alongside Curtin’s Artificial Intelligence Policy and
the policies of publishers, funders and governments relevant to your research.
Operative at policy cut-off: Unverified

WHY THE EVIDENCE FITS MY SCORE
Curtin explicitly covers researchers using AI tools (N145 s.3.1.10, A008), requiring verification of AI inputs/outputs (N001 Principle 8), author responsibility for accuracy and attribution (N145 s.3.3.7, N210), and protection of confidential research data (N001 Principle 4, N017 s.2.5).

Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has

Principle 7: Accountability
Implement human oversight, review and the ability to intervene for all AI-

decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.
Principle 8: Accuracy
Check inputs to and outputs from AI to provide assurance that they are
factual, reliable and as free from bias as practicable. Disclose limitations in
models that are known or likely.

Principle 5: Reliability and safety
Operate AI systems in accordance with the approved and intended purpose
and monitor their performance. Adopt safety measures proportionate to the
magnitude of potential risks.
Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has
been used and contest decisions made using AI.
Principle 7: Accountability
Implement human oversight, review and the ability to intervene for all AI-
based functions and decisions. Identify the individual accountable for any
decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.

Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has
been used and contest decisions made using AI.
Principle 7: Accountability
Implement human oversight, review and the ability to intervene for all AI-
based functions and decisions. Identify the individual accountable for any
decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.

Principle 4: Privacy protection and security
Comply with applicable data protection, security and privacy policies and
legislation. Adhere to the University’s architecture standards if incorporating
AI systems into the University’s enterprise information technology systems.
Principle 5: Reliability and safety
Operate AI systems in accordance with the approved and intended purpose
and monitor their performance. Adopt safety measures proportionate to the
magnitude of potential risks.
Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has
been used and contest decisions made using AI.
Principle 7: Accountability

based functions and decisions. Identify the individual accountable for any
decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.

Source ID: N001

Printed bundle page: 6

Evidence type: Exact quotation
Exact quotation:
Principle 4: Privacy protection and security
Comply with applicable data protection, security and privacy policies and
legislation. Adhere to the University’s architecture standards if incorporating
AI systems into the University’s enterprise information technology systems.
Principle 5: Reliability and safety
Operate AI systems in accordance with the approved and intended purpose
and monitor their performance. Adopt safety measures proportionate to the
magnitude of potential risks.
Principle 6: Transparency, Explainability and Contestability
Develop, deploy and use AI systems in a way that maximises transparency,
explainability and contestability. Allow stakeholders to know when AI has
been used and contest decisions made using AI.
Principle 7: Accountability
Implement human oversight, review and the ability to intervene for all AI-
based functions and decisions. Identify the individual accountable for any
decision made using AI. Maintain records on the use of AI to comply with
recordkeeping obligations.
